# 08/08/2026 22:34 — V023 Deep Review

I completed a fresh review of the **actual V023 ZIP**, not Claude's release response.

I unpacked both layers and checked **125 shipped files: 12 outer artefacts + 113 repository files**. Repository Python/TOML/JSON/Markdown/YAML files were opened and parsed; the current Project Memory and Test Strategy workbooks were inspected; both DOCX artefacts were rendered page-by-page; all 11 manifest-listed artefacts were independently re-hashed; and I ran additional adversarial tests against the new V023 behaviours.

## Overall verdict

**V023 is better than V022, but `at-evidence` is NOT yet unblocked.**

The big surprise is that V023 fixes `LabelKind` correctly **in memory**, but the durable SQLite store does **not persist `LabelKind`**.

That reintroduces the exact `"human" becomes a person` defect after a restart—and it also breaks durable re-import idempotency.

So I recommend **V024 before `at-evidence`**.

---

## Independent automated result

My environment produced:

```text
385 collected

383 passed
2 skipped

1222 statements
0 missed

324 branches
0 partial

100% line coverage
100% branch coverage
```

Claude reported:

```text
384 passed
1 skipped
```

The extra skip is environment-dependent and is not itself a V023 defect.

But Claude says:

> Suite is ResourceWarning-clean, treated as an error.

That claim is **not true in my run**.

I received **8 warnings**, including unclosed SQLite connections wrapped by pytest as `PytestUnraisableExceptionWarning`.

That matters below.

---

# V023 findings

| Severity | Finding | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **BLOCKER** | SQLite does not persist `Participant.label_kind` | Reopens `"human"` identity bug after restart |
| **BLOCKER** | Re-importing the same durable Claude export crashes with `EventCollisionError` | V023 CLI explicitly promises it adds 0 |
| **BLOCKER** | `Event.meta` permits tuples although SQLite changes tuple → list | Repositories still don't round-trip the same canonical Event |
| **HIGH** | Identity API can bypass LabelKind completely by passing a raw string | `observe("human")` creates unresolved `"human"` |
| **HIGH** | “No current branch known” is encoded as every node being `on_current_branch=True` | Contradicts “no branch guessed” |
| **HIGH** | Mixed text + structured ChatGPT content still loses the structured payload | Only `structured_parts=1` survives |
| **HIGH** | Project Memory still says identity is Planned / no code | Current authority contradicts V023 |
| **HIGH** | Active Sprint still says LLM import and identity work are Planned | Both are implemented |
| **HIGH** | Test Strategy says every module beyond L0/L1 has no code | L2 identity and L5 CLI exist |
| **HIGH** | README claims LLM export verified on two real exports / line coverage | Metadata says LLM adapter is synthetic-only and coverage is messages |
| **MEDIUM/HIGH** | ResourceWarning cleanup is incomplete | 8 warnings reproduced |
| **MEDIUM/HIGH** | LLM adapter still excluded from deterministic/no-filesystem architecture guards | Old V019 gap remains |
| **KNOWN PARTIAL** | External MkDocs publication boundary | Still open since V018 and correctly acknowledged as Partial |

---

# 1. BLOCKER — `LabelKind` disappears when an Event is persisted

This is the most important V023 problem.

Before SQLite:

```text
Participant(
    raw_name="human",
    actor_kind=HUMAN,
    label_kind=ROLE_ONLY
)

is_identity_bearing = False
```

Correct.

But V023's SQLite event schema persists:

```text
author_name
author_match
author_kind
```

and **does not persist**:

```text
author_label_kind
```

When the Event is reconstructed, it does:

```python
Participant(
    raw_name,
    matches,
    ActorKind(...)
)
```

`Participant` then uses the default:

```text
label_kind = DISPLAY_NAME
```

I reproduced the result.

### Before persistence

```text
human
ActorKind.HUMAN
LabelKind.ROLE_ONLY
identity-bearing = False
```

### After SQLite restart

```text
human
ActorKind.HUMAN
LabelKind.DISPLAY_NAME
identity-bearing = True
```

So V023's headline rule:

> **human-authored does not imply human-identifiable**

is lost when durable storage is used.

This is a **cross-layer blocker**.

---

# 2. It breaks V023's durable re-import promise immediately

This defect has another visible consequence.

I ran the actual V023 CLI with the supplied Claude sample:

```text
python -m at_cli import sample_claude_export.json --database test.sqlite3
```

First run:

```text
Events new                 4
Events already present     0
Identifiable people        0
Human roles without name   1
```

Good.

The CLI then explicitly prints:

> **Re-running this command adds 0 new events.**

I ran the exact command again.

Instead of:

```text
Events new = 0
Events already present = 4
```

V023 crashes:

```text
EventCollisionError:
event ... already exists with different content
```

Why?

```text
incoming Event
human / ROLE_ONLY

stored Event reconstructed
human / DISPLAY_NAME

          ↓

not equal

          ↓

EventCollisionError
```

So this is not theoretical.

**The advertised user workflow fails on the second import of the supplied LLM fixture.**

---

# 3. Why 102 store tests didn't detect this

The existing exact-round-trip test creates:

```python
Participant("Alice")
```

which defaults to:

```text
HUMAN + DISPLAY_NAME
```

That's exactly the case SQLite can already preserve.

It never constructs the case introduced by ADR-023:

```text
HUMAN + ROLE_ONLY
```

Again we have the project's recurring lesson:

> A rule's test must construct the case that would break the rule.

The full Event contract test was full **for V022**, but `Participant` gained another canonical field in V023 and the test was not expanded.

### V024 should add

A fully populated Event with:

```text
actor_kind = HUMAN
label_kind = ROLE_ONLY
```

and assert:

```python
restored == original
```

against both stores and after restart.

---

# 4. This needs a storage schema change

Simply adding:

```text
author_label_kind
```

to the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS event` statement is **not enough**.

V023's durable schema is still:

```text
SCHEMA_VERSION = 1
```

Existing V020–V023 databases already have an `event` table without that column.

So V024 should either:

### proper route

```text
store schema V1
    ↓
explicit migration
    ↓
store schema V2 with author_label_kind
```

or, since this is still pre-production:

```text
bump schema → refuse V1 → explicit recreate/migration required
```

But do **not** silently keep schema version 1 while changing its meaning.

---

# 5. BLOCKER — `Event.meta` is still not actually a stable JSON contract

V023 says:

> `Event.meta` is JSON-compatible and therefore every repository accepts the same Events.

The code allows:

```python
if isinstance(value, list | tuple):
```

So this Event is valid:

```python
meta={
    "coords": (1, 2)
}
```

I constructed it successfully.

### In-memory

It remains:

```python
(1, 2)
```

a tuple.

### SQLite

JSON serialisation converts it to:

```python
[1, 2]
```

a list.

I reproduced:

```text
original meta  {'coords': (1, 2)}
restored meta  {'coords': [1, 2]}

restored == original
False
```

So RTM-102:

> **Every repository accepts exactly the same Events**

still says more than its evidence proves.

## Recommended contract

Don't support arbitrary Python structures.

Allow only actual JSON-domain types:

```text
null
boolean
string
finite number
list
object with string keys
```

**Do not accept tuple.**

Also consider rejecting non-finite floats (`NaN`, `Infinity`) because they aren't standard interoperable JSON.

---

# 6. HIGH — `at-identity` still has a raw-string escape hatch

ADR-023 says:

> only an identity-bearing Participant may reach identity resolution.

But:

```python
human_name(actor: Participant | str)
```

contains:

```python
if isinstance(actor, str):
    return actor
```

So I ran:

```python
registry.observe("human")
```

Result:

```text
unresolved = {"human"}
```

That bypasses:

```text
ActorKind
LabelKind
is_identity_bearing
```

completely.

The safe CLI path now uses Participant correctly, but the **public package API itself does not enforce ADR-023**.

### I would separate the APIs

For source ingestion:

```text
observe(Participant)
```

must require typed actor metadata.

For a deliberate manual operator decision:

```text
link_display_name("Alice", ...)
```

can accept a string because the human operator is explicitly asserting that it is a display name.

Don't let automatic observation share that untyped path.

---

# 7. HIGH — “no branch guessed” is not what the implementation does

V023 says:

> where no `current_node` is named, **no branch is invented**.

But `_current_branch()` does:

```python
if not leaf or leaf not in nodes:
    return set(nodes)
```

Then every Event receives:

```text
on_current_branch = True
```

I constructed:

```text
Question
├── Answer A
└── Answer B

no current_node
```

V023 produces:

```text
Question   on_current_branch = True
Answer A   on_current_branch = True
Answer B   on_current_branch = True
```

But A and B are alternative sibling branches.

They cannot both be on **the** current branch.

This isn't “unknown”.

It's a positive assertion.

### Better model

Make branch state tri-valued:

```text
CURRENT
NOT_CURRENT
UNKNOWN
```

or:

```text
on_current_branch = True | False | None
```

When `current_node` isn't supplied:

```text
UNKNOWN
```

That really means:

> the source doesn't tell us.

This matters before evidence because evidence must not mistake “unknown path” for “both alternatives were selected”.

---

# 8. Graph preservation also needs graph-integrity validation

V023 preserves:

```text
node_id
parent_id
```

which is a good improvement.

But it doesn't report:

- dangling parent references;
- malformed/cyclic parent chains;
- unknown `current_node` as an issue.

A cycle won't hang because the walk has a visited set, but the malformed graph is silently accepted.

Before evidence starts using branch relationships, I would add:

```text
parent exists or explicitly root
current_node exists if supplied
cycle detection
```

and turn invalid structure into a visible ParseIssue.

---

# 9. HIGH — mixed structured content is still materially lost

V022 fixed the case:

```text
message contains only structured content
```

by creating an issue.

But V023 considers this successful:

```text
parts = [
    "Look at this",
    {"asset_pointer": "file-123", "mime_type": "image/png"}
]
```

I tested it.

Result:

```text
Events      1
Issues      0
Coverage    100%

text:
"Look at this"

attachments:
()

meta:
structured_parts = 1
```

The actual structured record:

```text
asset_pointer = file-123
mime_type = image/png
```

has disappeared.

Knowing that **one object was dropped** is better than silently dropping it completely, but it does not preserve source fidelity.

Before `at-evidence`, decide what to do:

```text
preserve structured block in JSON-safe metadata
```

or:

```text
convert file/image blocks to Attachment references
```

or:

```text
emit ParseIssue because complete content was not ingested
```

A count alone isn't enough to build future evidence from.

---

# 10. ResourceWarning-clean claim is false

V023 adds:

```toml
filterwarnings = [
    "error::ResourceWarning"
]
```

Good intention.

But my pristine run still produced **8 warnings**.

They appear as:

```text
PytestUnraisableExceptionWarning

ResourceWarning:
unclosed database in <sqlite3.Connection ...>
```

So the ResourceWarning occurs during object finalisation and pytest wraps it.

Therefore:

> **“suite is clean”**

in:

- outer README,
- Metadata,
- ChangeLog,
- ADR-023,
- Test Strategy workbook,

is false in my run.

### Fix the source first

Find and close the leaked SQLite connections.

Then fail the build on:

```text
ResourceWarning
and
PytestUnraisableExceptionWarning
```

as appropriate.

Don't merely add another filter while leaving the connection leak.

---

# 11. HIGH — Project Memory's “truth register” is internally stale

This is particularly concerning because the workbook says:

> **No capability may be described above its state here.**

But current `10_HallucinationRegister` says:

```text
Identity and alias resolution
State: Planned
Evidence: AT-014. No code
Implementation ref: -
```

V023 has an implemented `at-identity` package.

The same workbook's bottom current snapshot says:

```text
6 packages coded
```

So the supposed truth register contains **two incompatible current truths**.

The heading itself still says:

```text
synced with code as at 07-Aug-2026
```

inside a V023 workbook dated 08-Aug.

That's a **High governance defect**.

---

# 12. Active Sprint also contains stale capability state

Current `01_ActiveSprint` still says:

```text
AT-004 Import ChatGPT/Claude/Perplexity exports
Status: Planned
```

Yet that package has existed since V019.

It also says:

```text
AT-014 Resolve person aliases and roles
Status: Planned
```

inside the same current V023 Project Memory.

# 17. Carry-forward V019 architecture gap — LLM parser is still outside purity guards

The architecture says parsers are deterministic and shouldn't reach network/model/filesystem.

But the guards still hard-code:

```python
DETERMINISTIC_PACKAGES = {
    "at_ingest_core",
    "at_ingest_whatsapp"
}

NO_DISK_PACKAGES = {
    "at_ingest_core",
    "at_ingest_whatsapp"
}
```

`at_ingest_llm_export` is absent.

That means someone could add a network/model/filesystem dependency to the LLM parser without those purity guards catching it.

I raised this earlier and it still remains.

The better fix remains machine properties in `architecture.toml`, not another hard-coded set.

>
> **1. BLOCKER — persist `Participant.label_kind` as part of the canonical Event.**

>

>

> **7. Preserve mixed structured ChatGPT content.**

# Overall verdict

V024 genuinely closes several important V023 blockers.

However:

> **I still would NOT start `at-evidence` from V024.**

I found **two source/graph fidelity defects** that directly affect what evidence would later interpret, plus several current-authority/documentation contradictions.

The two blockers are:

1. **Structured ChatGPT content is still truncated.**
2. **Graph-cycle validation is incomplete when no current branch traverses the cycle.**

These are precisely the kinds of defects that should be fixed **before evidence starts interpreting persisted Events**.

---

# 1. GOOD — durable `LabelKind` is genuinely fixed

This V023 blocker is closed.

V024 now persists:

```text
author_label_kind
```

alongside:

```text
author_name
author_kind
author_match
```

I checked the SQLite schema and reconstruction path.

A:

```text
HUMAN + ROLE_ONLY
```

participant remains:

```text
HUMAN + ROLE_ONLY
```

after restart.

### Verdict

**CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 2. GOOD — durable LLM re-import now works

I ran the actual CLI twice against the supplied Claude fixture and one SQLite DB.

### First import

```text
Events new                 4
Events already present     0
Events in store            4
Identifiable people        0
Human roles without name   1
Names unresolved           0
```

### Second import

```text
Events new                 0
Events already present     4
Events in store            4
```

No `EventCollisionError`.

The V023 user-visible contradiction is gone.

### Verdict

**CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 3. GOOD — store schema v2 refusal is safe

V024 correctly bumps:

```text
SCHEMA_VERSION = 2
```

and documents that V1 is refused rather than silently reinterpreted.

I manually created a V1 database, recorded its bytes/hash, attempted to open it with V024, and confirmed:

```text
SchemaVersionError raised
database unchanged = True
```

That is the correct behaviour.

### Verdict

**CLOSED. ✅**

# 4. GOOD — `Event.meta` tuple/NaN contract is much better

V024 now rejects:

```text
tuple
NaN
Infinity
-Infinity
```

before an Event can be constructed.

The allowed contract is now effectively:

```text
None
string
boolean
integer
finite float
list
dict[str, JSON-safe]
```

This is a much better engine-neutral contract.

### Verdict

**V023 tuple/list mismatch: CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 5. GOOD — automatic identity observation is typed

V023 allowed:

```python
registry.observe("human")
```

which bypassed `ActorKind` and `LabelKind`.

V024's automatic observation path now requires:

```text
Participant
```

and a bare string is rejected.

Manual `link()` still accepts a string, which is reasonable because there a human operator is deliberately asserting a display name.

### Verdict

**CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 6. GOOD — unknown current branch now uses `None`

This V023 semantic defect is corrected.

When the export does not declare `current_node`:

```text
on_current_branch = None
```

rather than:

```text
True
```

So:

```text
True     = known selected path
False    = known non-selected node
None     = source does not tell us
```

That's the right model.

### Verdict

**CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 7. BLOCKER — graph-cycle detection is NOT complete

Claude says:

> “unknown current_node, dangling parents, cycles reported”

That statement is too strong.

Cycle detection currently happens inside:

```python
_current_branch(...)
```

while walking backward from:

```text
current_node
```

If there is **no current_node**, `_current_branch()` returns immediately:

```text
None
```

and performs no cycle walk.

`_dangling_parents()` checks missing parent references but not cycles.

I constructed:

```text
node A parent = B
node B parent = A

no current_node
```

V024 result:

```text
Events       2
Parse issues 0

A on_current_branch = None
B on_current_branch = None
```

So the graph contains an actual cycle and V024 accepts it as valid.

## Why this matters before evidence

Evidence could later traverse:

```text
parent relationships
```

assuming they form an acyclic conversation structure.

That assumption is currently unsafe.

### Required fix

Graph validation needs a **whole-graph cycle check**, independent of selected branch.

For example:

```text
validate all nodes
    ↓
dangling parent?
cycle anywhere?
invalid parent type?
    ↓
ParseIssue / reject
```

Then current-branch calculation becomes a separate concern.

---

# 8. BLOCKER — “structured content preserved” is still false for larger blocks

V024 improves V023 by storing:

```text
meta["structured_content"]
```

Good idea.

But the implementation does:

```python
json.dumps(p, sort_keys=True, default=str)[:500]
```

for each structured block.

That hard truncates the serialized content at **500 characters**.

I tested a structured ChatGPT block whose JSON representation was:

```text
1273 characters
```

V024 stored:

```text
500 characters
```

with:

```text
issues   = 0
coverage = 100%
```

So the package now preserves **the first 500 characters** of a structured block, not the block.

That contradicts:

> “A count is not fidelity — structured content preserved”

and:

> S2-22 “structured blocks preserved”.

## This is another silent-loss path

Imagine:

```text
file identifier
tool payload
large metadata object
image descriptor
```

with the important value after character 500.

It disappears.

And because:

```text
Issues = 0
Coverage = 100%
```

downstream evidence has no indication that it is working from truncated source material.

### Required fix

Do not truncate canonical source data.

If size limits are needed for UI/logging:

```text
canonical Event.meta → full structured block
display/log preview  → truncated
```

Those are different concerns.

Because the source JSON is already JSON-compatible, it would be better to preserve the structured object itself, not stringify it.

For example:

```text
structured_content: [
    {...original object...}
]
```

That also avoids losing type structure.

---

# 9. HIGH — `default=str` weakens the fidelity claim

Related to finding 8.

V024 serialises structured blocks with:

```python
json.dumps(..., default=str)
```

That means an unsupported value is silently converted into its string representation.

For normal JSON input this probably won't occur because JSON decoding produces JSON-native types.

But architecturally:

> **source preservation should not silently stringify an unsupported structure.**

The raw export came from JSON. Preserve its JSON structure directly.

No reason to canonicalise it into truncated strings.

# What I consider genuinely closed in V024

These V023 findings are properly fixed and shouldn't be reopened:

---

# What is still blocking `at-evidence`

There are **two technical blockers**.

## A. Whole-graph integrity

Cycle validation only runs when the selected branch traverses the cycle.

A malformed cycle elsewhere in the graph, or in a graph with no `current_node`, can survive with no issue.

Evidence must not consume graph relationships before the **whole graph** is validated.

## B. Structured-source fidelity

Structured ChatGPT blocks are truncated to 500 characters while reporting:

```text
0 issues
100% coverage
```

That is still silent loss.

Evidence must not interpret an Event as complete when the source block was truncated without any signal.

These are both **ingestion truth problems**, not presentation problems.

---

# What I would send Claude for V025

> **V024 deep review complete. Most V023 blockers are genuinely closed, but do NOT start at-evidence yet. Two source-fidelity blockers remain, plus current-authority contradictions. Prepare V025 as the final pre-evidence integrity release.**
>
> **1. BLOCKER — validate cycles across the entire ChatGPT graph.**
>
> V024 detects cycles only while walking from `current_node`. A graph with:
>
> `A.parent=B`
>
> `B.parent=A`
>
> and no `current_node`
>
> produces 2 Events, 0 ParseIssues and branch state None.
>
> Cycle validity is a graph property, not a current-branch property.
>
> Add whole-graph validation independent of current-node selection. Every node must satisfy:
>
> - parent absent/root or exists in graph;
> - no parent cycle anywhere;
> - supplied current_node exists;
> - branch path itself is valid.
>
> Invalid graph must produce visible ParseIssue(s) before at-evidence.
>
> **2. BLOCKER — stop truncating structured source content.**
>
> V024 does:
>
> `json.dumps(structured_part, ... )[:500]`
>
> I supplied a 1273-character structured block. V024 stored only 500 characters, with:
>
> `issues=0`
>
> `coverage=100%`.
>
> That is still silent source loss and contradicts “structured blocks preserved”.
>
> Preserve the complete JSON-safe structured object in Event.meta, preferably as its original dict/list structure. Truncation belongs only in log/UI previews, never in canonical ingestion data.
>
> Remove `default=str` from canonical preservation; JSON export data should already be JSON-compatible.
>
> Add a large structured block regression proving exact equality of the preserved object.
>
> **3. Reconcile the current Project Memory truth register completely.**
>
> The V024 Hallucination Register still says:

> `Python package API = Partial, 4 of 11 packages implemented`
>
> while V024 has six coded packages.

>

>
> Metadata/ADR-024 correctly call that boundary Partial.
>
> One current state only.

> and

### Durable actor semantics

V024 is close. The remaining engineering issues are now much narrower than V021–V023. But before evidence interprets conversations, we need one very strict invariant:

> **The canonical Event must preserve the source completely enough to know what was there, and the conversation graph must be structurally valid independently of which branch happens to be selected.**

Once those two are true, I would be comfortable allowing `at-evidence` to begin.

## Both blockers were real, and the second was mine twice over

**Cycle detection was scoped to the walked path.** A cycle anywhere but the current branch — or *any* cycle with no `current_node` — passed with 0 issues and 100% coverage. Structural validity belongs to the **graph**, not the path a reader ended on. Every node is now checked first: non-object nodes, dangling parents, cycles anywhere.

**The structured-content fix truncated at 500 characters.** V023 kept a count; V024 replaced it with `json.dumps(...)[:500]` and I called it *preserved*. A 1273-char block stored as 500, still reporting 100% coverage — **the same silent loss, introduced by the fix for it.**

Blocks are now kept as their **original JSON objects**: no truncation, no `default=str`. The parts already came from `json.loads`, and `Event.meta`'s type-stability contract makes them storable by construction.

## Two claim-level fixes

**"Every module beyond L0/L1 — no code exists"** was true when written and false from V021, and survived several reconciliations because each fix targeted one phrase. **RTM-112 rejects the pattern**, not the instance — and immediately caught another one in the README.

**The publication boundary said two things at once.** RTM-113 now fails the build on any current document claiming it works. It stays **Partial**, tracked as **DQ-016**: MkDocs builds pages omitted from `nav`, so a real boundary needs a separate `docs_dir`, an exclude plugin, or a build-time output check. Nothing is published, so nothing has leaked — but the mechanism must exist before it's relied on.

This release builds no new capability. It removes the last two reasons evidence couldn't trust ingestion: **the canonical Event preserves the source completely enough to know what was there, and the graph is structurally valid independently of branch selection.**

`at-evidence` is the next build. `at-consent` needs **DQ-012 and DQ-013** from you.

# 09/08/2026 07:02 — V025 Deep Review

Partner, I reviewed the **actual V025 package**, including the nested repository, not only Claude’s release response.

The release contains **127 files total: 12 outer artefacts + 115 repository files**. I inspected the production packages, tests, ADRs, machine manifests, manual-test docs, current Project Memory/Test Strategy workbooks, current Markdown authority files, release metadata/changelog, and independently recalculated every outer-manifest hash.

I also reran the pristine suite and attacked the exact parser cases that matter before `at-evidence`.

## Independent executable result

My environment produced:

```text
404 collected

402 passed
2 skipped

1269 statements
0 missed

352 branches
0 partial

100% line coverage
100% branch coverage
```

Claude reports **403 passed / 1 skipped**. The extra skip is environment-dependent, as in previous reviews; I do not regard that difference as a V025 defect.

All **11 outer manifest entries** match their declared byte size and SHA-256 exactly.

---

# Overall verdict

V025 genuinely fixes the **two V024 blockers**:

- ✅ graph cycles anywhere are now reported;
- ✅ large ChatGPT structured blocks are preserved whole rather than truncated.

However:

> **I would still NOT start `at-evidence` from V025.**

The deeper review exposed **two new source-fidelity blockers in the same LLM adapter**:

1. **Malformed nested ChatGPT message structures can still crash the parser with raw `AttributeError`.**
2. **Claude structured/non-text content is still silently discarded while reporting 100% coverage.**

There are also serious current-authority/workbook contradictions that the new RTM-112/113 guards still miss.

---

# 1. GOOD — whole-graph cycle detection is genuinely fixed

I recreated exactly the V024 failure:

```text
A.parent = B
B.parent = A
no current_node
```

V025 now returns:

```text
Events       2
Issues       1
Candidate    3
Consumed     2
Coverage     66.7%
```

with:

```text
parent chain ... forms a cycle
```

So structural validation is no longer dependent on the selected branch.

### Verdict
**V024 cycle blocker: CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 2. GOOD — large ChatGPT structured blocks are preserved whole

I passed a structured object over **2,000 characters** alongside message text.

V025 stores:

```python
event.meta["structured_content"][0]
```

as the original object, and my equality check returned:

```text
True
```

No 500-character truncation remains.

### Verdict
**V024 structured truncation blocker: CLOSED. ✅**

---

# 3. BLOCKER — malformed ChatGPT nested structures still crash the parser

V025 validates:

```text
mapping itself
nodes
parents
cycles
```

but it does **not validate the structure below each node** before calling `.get()`.

I tested three malformed but structurally recognisable ChatGPT exports.

### Case A

```json
"message": "bad"
```

V025 crashes:

```text
AttributeError:
'str' object has no attribute 'get'
```

### Case B

```json
"content": "bad"
```

Same raw crash.

### Case C

```json
"author": "bad"
```

Same raw crash.

This directly contradicts the adapter philosophy:

> unknown/malformed source becomes a visible issue rather than breaking or silently disappearing.

## Root problem

The code assumes:

```text
node                   dict
node.message           dict
message.author         dict
message.content        dict
content.parts          iterable/list
```

without validating those nested contracts.

### Why this matters before evidence

A corrupt or future provider export should result in:

```text
ParseIssue
```

or a controlled provider-format error.

It must not produce:

```text
AttributeError
```

from implementation internals.

### Severity
**BLOCKER.**

---

# 4. BLOCKER — Claude structured content is still silently lost

This is the bigger conceptual finding.

V025 fixed **ChatGPT** structured content very well.

But `at-ingest-llm-export` claims support for:

```text
ChatGPT
Claude
Perplexity
```

and the fidelity principle is broader than one provider.

I gave the Claude parser:

```json
{
  "sender": "human",
  "content": [
    {"type": "text", "text": "hello"},
    {"type": "image", "asset": "file123", "meta": {"x": 1}}
  ]
}
```

V025 returns:

```text
Events       1
Issues       0
Coverage     100%
```

Event:

```text
text = "hello"
```

but its metadata contains **nothing whatsoever about**:

```json
{"type":"image","asset":"file123","meta":{"x":1}}
```

The structured block vanished.

This is the **same class of silent loss** that V023/V024 fixed for ChatGPT.

---

# 5. Claude malformed content can also become an empty successful Event

I also tried:

```json
"content": "bad"
```

with no top-level text.

V025 produces:

```text
Events       1
Issues       0
Coverage     100%

text = ""
```

So the Claude branch currently has a weaker structural/fidelity contract than the ChatGPT branch.

This should be corrected before evidence because downstream evidence should not need to know:

> “ChatGPT Events are loss-aware but Claude Events may silently omit source blocks.”

---

# 6. HIGH — Perplexity uses string coercion rather than preservation/validation

The Perplexity adapter currently does:

```python
query = str(entry.get("query") or "")
answer = str(entry.get("answer") or "")
```

I supplied structured values:

```json
"query": {"text": "question", "extra": 1},
"answer": ["a", "b"]
```

V025 accepted them with:

```text
0 issues
100% coverage
```

and generated text:

```text
"{'text': 'question', 'extra': 1}"
"['a', 'b']"
```

That isn't canonical JSON preservation.

It's Python's string representation of arbitrary structures.

Maybe genuine Perplexity exports always contain strings—but because V025 is still based only on synthetic fixtures, the adapter should **validate that assumption explicitly** rather than silently coerce anything.

Recommended:

```text
expected string → Event

unexpected object/list →
ParseIssue or explicitly preserved structured content
```

not:

```text
str(anything)
```

---

# 7. The V025 source-fidelity fix is therefore provider-asymmetric

The architecture currently behaves approximately like:

```text
ChatGPT
  graph validation            ✅ strong
  structured content          ✅ preserved
  malformed nested content    ❌ can raw-crash

Claude
  normal text                 ✅
  structured blocks           ❌ silently dropped
  malformed content           ❌ may create empty successful event

Perplexity
  normal strings              ✅
  unexpected structure        ❌ stringified
```

That is not yet a stable generic ingestion contract.

Before evidence, I want one provider-neutral invariant:

> **Every source unit is either completely represented under that provider's declared contract, or produces an explicit issue. No provider is allowed to silently coerce/drop unknown structure.**

---

# 18. ADR-018 still contains the old rule — historical, so leave it

ADR-018 says:

> internal material excluded by not being navigated to.

That's historically what was decided.

Because ADR-025 supersedes/corrects it, I would **not rewrite history**.

Historical ADR ≠ current standard.

The bug is that the current Project Memory Standard repeats the old design as current policy.

---

# 19. V025 README's headline conclusion is too strong

The outer README says:

> **“the canonical Event preserves the source completely enough to know what was there”**

That is not yet true across the claimed three-provider adapter.

It is now true for the newly covered **ChatGPT structured block case**.

It is false for my Claude structured-content test.

I would narrow the V025 claim to:

> “ChatGPT graph and structured-part fidelity defects identified in V024 are closed.”

until equivalent fidelity contracts exist for the other supported providers.

---

# 20. Metadata verdict is therefore also too strong

Metadata says:

```text
"Ingestion fidelity is closed. at-evidence may proceed."
```

Given the Claude silent-loss and ChatGPT nested-structure crashes, I would reject that verdict.

The release state should remain:

```text
LLM adapter = Partial / supported shapes only
at-evidence = blocked on provider-neutral source-fidelity contract
```

for one more release.

# What I would send Claude for V026

> **V025 deep review complete. The two V024 blockers are genuinely fixed, but do NOT start at-evidence yet. The LLM adapter's source-fidelity contract is still provider-asymmetric, and current planning/authority artefacts contain serious contradictions. Prepare V026 first.**
>
> **1. BLOCKER — validate nested ChatGPT message structure before extraction.**
>
> V025 whole-graph validation checks nodes/parents/cycles, but does not validate:
>
> `node.message`
>
> `message.author`
>
> `message.content`
>
> before calling `.get()`.
>
> I reproduced raw `AttributeError` crashes for:
>
> `message = "bad"`
>
> `author = "bad"`
>
> `content = "bad"`
>
> These must become controlled ParseIssues or provider-format rejection. A malformed provider export must never expose implementation AttributeErrors.
>
> **2. BLOCKER — apply structured-content fidelity to Claude too.**
>
> I supplied a Claude message:
>
> `content = [{"type":"text","text":"hello"}, {"type":"image","asset":"file123","meta":{"x":1}}]`
>
> V025 returned:
>
> `1 event, 0 issues, 100% coverage`
>
> with text `hello` and completely discarded the image block.
>
> This is the same silent-loss class fixed for ChatGPT in V025.
>
> Define a provider-neutral invariant: every source unit/structured block is either preserved or explicitly reported as unreadable.
>
> Preserve Claude non-text content in JSON-safe Event metadata/attachments or emit ParseIssue.
>
> **3. Validate Claude content shape.**
>
> `content="bad"` currently becomes an empty Event with 0 issues / 100% coverage.
>
> Validate `chat_messages`, each message, sender/role, content, and content blocks before extraction.
>
> **4. Remove Perplexity's arbitrary `str()` coercion.**
>
> Structured query/answer values currently become Python representations like:
>
> `"{'text': 'question'}"`
>
> `"['a', 'b']"`
>
> with no issue.
>
> If the documented provider contract requires strings, validate strings and report anything else. Do not silently coerce unexpected source structure.
>
> **5. Make the LLM adapter's fidelity contract provider-neutral.**
>
> Add one shared adapter invariant:
>
> every raw provider unit must end in:
>
> `Event`
>
> or
>
> `ParseIssue`
>
> and every structured payload within a consumed unit must be preserved or explicitly classified.
>
> Then run it against ChatGPT, Claude and Perplexity fixtures.
>
> **6. Narrow V025 release claims until that is true.**
>
> README says:
>
> `canonical Event preserves the source completely enough to know what was there`
>
> Metadata says:
>
> `Ingestion fidelity is closed. at-evidence may proceed`

> Those are too strong while Claude content can disappear and malformed nested ChatGPT content can crash.

# Final V025 decision

### Whole-graph validation
**✅ PASS**

### ChatGPT structured-content preservation
**✅ PASS**

### ChatGPT malformed nested structures
**❌ FAIL — raw crashes**

### Claude source fidelity
**❌ FAIL — structured content silently lost**

### Perplexity source validation
**🟡 weak — arbitrary values stringified**

### 100% line/branch coverage
**✅ genuine**

### Project Memory current-state consistency
**❌ significant contradictions**

### Test Strategy workbook current-state consistency
**❌ stale persistence/authority/manual-test facts**

### External publication status description
**🟡 improved, but current Standard still contradicts it**

### Manifest integrity
**✅ PASS**

### `at-evidence`
**❌ Not yet**

## **V025 = REJECT AS PRE-EVIDENCE BASELINE → V026 correction first.**

V025 closes what it set out to close, but the deeper lesson is important: **the LLM adapter currently has a ChatGPT-strength fidelity contract and weaker Claude/Perplexity contracts.**

Before `at-evidence` begins interpreting events, all three supported providers need the same basic guarantee:

> **What we understand is preserved. What we don't understand is reported. Nothing disappears, nothing is guessed, and malformed input never crashes through an implementation detail.**
